<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;We lease additional warehouse and office space in the United States, Hong Kong, Canada and
Latin America pursuant to long and short-term contracts. We also contract with third party
distribution providers that provide full service warehousing and shipping services. Applica has
such arrangements in Miami, Tennessee, Mississippi, Puerto Rico, Mexico, Colombia, Venezuela,
Chile, Peru, Argentina, Central America and China. Service contracts are typically short-term in
nature, with fixed pricing, and provide for specific performance requirements related to customer
service.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;We believe our current facilities are adequate to meet our needs in the foreseeable future. If
necessary, we may, from time to time, downsize current facilities or lease additional facilities
for warehousing and/or other activities.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In the third quarter of 2004, Applica sold its Hong Kong-based manufacturing operations and
transferred control of the related manufacturing facilities. In the fourth quarter of 2005,
Applica entered into an agreement to sell its manufacturing facilities located in Queretaro, Mexico
totaling 290,000 square feet. We expect the sale to close in the second quarter of 2006.
</DIV>
<!-- link2 "Item&nbsp;3. Legal Proceedings" -->

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Item&nbsp;3. Legal Proceedings</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Shareholder Litigation</I></B>. Applica is a defendant in <U>Scott Schultz, individually and on
behalf of all others similarly situated, v. Applica Incorporated, Harry D. Schulman and Terry L.
Polistina</U>, 06-60149-CIV-DIMITROULEAS, which was filed in the United States District Court,
Southern District of Florida on February&nbsp;3, 2006, and <U>Joseph Rothman, individually and on
behalf of all others similarly situated, v. Applica Incorporated, Harry D. Schulman and Terry L.
Polistina</U>, 06-60230-CIV-ZLOCH, which was filed in the United States District Court, Southern
District of Florida on February&nbsp;24, 2006.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;These matters are purported class action complaints filed on behalf of purchasers of Applica
Incorporated common stock during the period between November&nbsp;4, 2004 and April&nbsp;28, 2005. The
complaints charge Applica and certain executive officers with violations of the Securities Exchange
Act of 1934. The complaints allege that, throughout the class period, Applica issued materially
false and misleading statements regarding its ability to transform its business and become more
profitable. The complaints claim that these statements were materially false and misleading on the
asserted basis that they failed to disclose the following: (a)&nbsp;that Applica was experiencing
decreasing demand for its products; in particular, demand for two key products, Tide&#153; Buzz&#153;
Ultrasonic Stain Remover and Home Cafe&#153; single cup coffee maker, were not meeting internal
expectations; (b)&nbsp;that Applica was materially overstating its net worth by failing to timely write
down the value of its inventory which had become obsolete and unsaleable; (c)&nbsp;that Applica was
experiencing higher product warranty returns for which it had not
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">-16-
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">appropriately reserved; and (d)&nbsp;that Applica&#146;s financial statements issued during the class
period were not prepared in accordance with GAAP and therefore were materially false and
misleading.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The plaintiffs seek, among other relief, to be declared a class, to be awarded compensatory
damages, rescission rights, unspecified damages and attorneys&#146; fees and costs. Applica believes
the claims are without merit. We intend to vigorously defend these lawsuits but may be unable to
successfully resolve the disputes without incurring significant expenses. Due to the early stage
of these proceedings, any potential loss cannot presently be determined with respect to these
litigation matters.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As is often the case, the SEC has requested that Applica voluntarily produce certain documents
in connection with an informal inquiry related to these matters. We intend to respond to the
requests for documents and are fully cooperating with the SEC in this matter.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Other Matters</I></B>. Applica is subject to legal proceedings, products liability claims and other
claims that arise in the ordinary course of our business. In the opinion of management, the amount
of ultimate liability, if any, in excess of applicable insurance coverage, is not likely to have a
material effect on the financial condition, results of operations or liquidity of Applica.
However, as the outcome of litigation or other claims is difficult to predict, significant changes
in the estimated exposures could occur.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As a distributor of consumer products, Applica is also subject to the Consumer Products Safety
Act, which empowers the Consumer Products Safety Commission (CPSC)&nbsp;to exclude from the market
products that are found to be unsafe or hazardous. Applica receives inquiries from the CPSC in the
ordinary course of its business. In the opinion of management, the amount of ultimate liability
with respect to such matters, if any, is not likely to have a material effect on Applica&#146;s
business, financial condition, results of operations or liquidity. However, under certain
circumstances, the CPSC could require Applica to repurchase or recall one or more of its products.
</DIV>
<!-- link2 "Item&nbsp;4. Submission of Matters to a Vote of Security Holders" -->

</DIV>

the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2005.

<!-- link1 "PART II" -->

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 18pt"><B>PART II</B>
</DIV>

<!-- link2 "Item&nbsp;5. Market for the Registrant&#146;s Common Stock and Related Stockholder Matters" -->

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Item&nbsp;5. Market for the Registrant&#146;s Common Stock and Related Stockholder Matters</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica&#146;s common stock, $0.10 par value, is currently listed for trading on the New York Stock
Exchange under the symbol &#147;APN.&#148; In August&nbsp;2005, Applica was notified by the New York Stock
Exchange that it was not in compliance with the NYSE&#146;s continued listing standards. Applica is
considered &#147;below criteria&#148; by the NYSE because over a consecutive 30&nbsp;day trading period, its total
market capitalization and its shareholders&#146; equity were each less than $75&nbsp;million. While Applica
was in full compliance with the previous continued listing standards set forth by the Exchange, the
NYSE adopted new continued listing standards with which Applica is not currently in compliance.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In order to maintain the continued listing of our common stock on the NYSE, Applica is
following the NYSE&#146;s rules and procedures applicable to listed companies which fail to meet the
continued listing criteria. In September&nbsp;2005, Applica presented a plan to the NYSE demonstrating
how it intends to comply with the continued listing standards, which plan was accepted by the New
York Stock Exchange. As a result of the acceptance, Applica&#146;s common stock will continue to be
listed on the NYSE pending quarterly reviews by the NYSE&#146;s Listing and Compliance Committee to
ensure progress against the plan. We expect that the first review will be completed in April&nbsp;2006.
We cannot assure you that the NYSE will maintain our listing in the future.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The following tables set forth, for the periods indicated, the range of high and low closing
prices for the common stock as reported on the New York Stock Exchange:

March&nbsp;15, 2006, there were approximately 800 holders of record of our common stock. The

</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Dividends</I></B>. We are prohibited from declaring or paying cash dividends on our capital stock
under the terms of our debt agreements. We did not pay any common stock dividends in 2005 or 2004,
and we do not anticipate paying dividends in the foreseeable future. We intend to retain any
earnings to finance our operations and for general corporate purposes.

    <TD>Effective January&nbsp;1, 2005, Applica modified its accounting treatment for cooperative
advertising and slotting fees. This modification resulted in a reclassification that
reduced each of net sales, gross profit and selling, general and administrative expenses by
$13.8&nbsp;million in 2005, $17.0&nbsp;million in 2004, $17.2&nbsp;million in 2003, $21.5&nbsp;million in 2002
and $17.6&nbsp;million in 2001. The net sales figures presented above reflect such
reclassification. Because the modification resulted solely in a reclassification within
the consolidated statement of operations, there was no impact on Applica&#146;s financial
condition, operating income or net earnings for any periods presented.</TD>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Item&nbsp;7. Management&#146;s Discussion and Analysis of Financial Condition and Results of Operations</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 18pt"><B>Overview</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The following discussion of our financial conditions and results of operations should be read
in conjunction with our financial statements and the related notes included in Schedule&nbsp;I to this
Annual Report on Form 10-K. This discussion contains forward-looking statements. Please see Item
1A. Risk Factors, above, for a discussion of the uncertainties, risks and assumptions associated
with these statements.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica is a marketer and distributor of a broad range of branded small household appliances.
Applica markets and distributes kitchen products, home products, pest control products, pet care
products and personal care products. Applica markets products under licensed brand names, such as
Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP>, and its own brand names, such as LitterMaid<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP>, Belson<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP>, Windmere<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> and Applica<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP>.
Applica&#146;s customers include mass merchandisers, specialty retailers and appliance distributors
primarily in North America, Latin America and the Caribbean.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Through October&nbsp;2005, Applica managed its operations through three business segments:
Household Products, Professional Personal Care Products and Manufacturing. The Manufacturing
segment ceased operations in October&nbsp;2005. For additional information, see Note R, Business
Segment and Geographic Area Information, to the Consolidated Financial Statements included in
Schedule&nbsp;I to this Annual Report on Form 10-K.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;We continuously have to balance the cost of our products, without compromising quality, with
the price constraints from our customers. The prices of raw materials such as copper, steel and
plastics have significantly increased in recent years and are expected to remain high in the
foreseeable future. This has negatively impacted our gross margins by increasing the price we pay
for our products, which in many circumstances we have been unable to pass on to our customers. This
is expected to continue to negatively impact our margins in 2006.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Mexican Manufacturing Operations</I></B>. In recent years, we have been rationalizing our Mexican
manufacturing operations. In 2004 and the first quarter of 2005, we shifted a significant amount
of production from Mexico to third parties in China and began to reduce our Mexican manufacturing
capacity to reflect only the volume needed for the Mexican marketplace. In July&nbsp;2005, the decision
was made to close our manufacturing operations in Mexico and production ceased in October&nbsp;2005.
The decision resulted primarily from competitive pressures from Chinese manufacturers.
Manufacturers in China are now able to provide good quality and well-designed products at a cost
that is lower than our cost to produce a similar product in Mexico.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;With the closure of the Mexican facility, we currently outsource the manufacturing of all of
our products to third party suppliers located primarily in China. This allows us to concentrate
our efforts on marketing, distribution and sourcing of our products. We expect to see the benefits
of this decision in 2006 as we continue to work with our suppliers to obtain the lowest possible
product cost without compromising quality. We do not expect to fully realize gross margin
improvements from moving production to China until all of the remaining products manufactured by
our Mexican facility are sold through to customers.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In connection with the decision to close the Mexican manufacturing facility, we recorded a
total of $12.5&nbsp;million in restructuring charges in 2005. These charges consisted of the following:
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;All restructuring charges relate to the Manufacturing reportable segment.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;We auctioned a significant portion of the machinery and equipment, along with some office
furniture and equipment, in Mexico in December&nbsp;2005. As a result of the auction, we realized net
cash proceeds of approximately $2.9&nbsp;million. As of December&nbsp;31, 2005, we collected $2.0&nbsp;million of
such proceeds. The remaining proceeds of $0.9&nbsp;million were collected in February&nbsp;2006. The
December&nbsp;2005 auction resulted in a small gain. We expect to
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">-23-
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">have another auction in March&nbsp;2006 for the remaining property and equipment and expect to
realize net proceeds of approximately $0.4&nbsp;million. We do not expect the gain or loss associated
with the March auction to be significant.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In February&nbsp;2006, we entered into a sale agreement with an unrelated third party to sell the
land and building housing our factory in Mexico. We expect the sale to close in the second quarter
of 2006. The land and building were classified as an asset held for sale and included in prepaid
expenses and other in the accompanying consolidated balance sheets. The value of the facilities
was approximately $5.3&nbsp;million (net of commissions) as of December&nbsp;31, 2005. We do not expect a
gain or loss upon the sale of the land and building.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Use of Estimates and Critical Accounting Policies</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In preparing financial statements in conformity with accounting principles generally accepted
in the United States of America, management is required to make estimates and assumptions that
affect the reported amounts of assets and liabilities and the disclosure of contingent assets and
liabilities at the date of the financial statements and revenues and expenses during the reporting
period. Actual results could differ from those estimates. Significant estimates include income
taxes, the allowance for doubtful accounts, inventory valuation reserves, product liability,
depreciation and amortization.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Management believes that the following may involve a higher degree of judgment or complexity:
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<U><B><I>Income Taxes</I></B></U>. Applica is subject to income tax laws in many countries. Judgment is
required in assessing the future tax consequences of events that have been recognized in Applica&#146;s
financial statements and tax returns. Significant management judgment is required in developing
Applica&#146;s provision for income taxes, including the determination of foreign tax liabilities,
deferred tax assets and liabilities and any valuation allowances that might be required to be
applied against the deferred tax assets. Applica evaluates its ability to realize its deferred tax
assets on a quarterly basis and adjusts the amount of its valuation allowance, if necessary.
Applica operates within multiple taxing jurisdictions and is subject to audit in those
jurisdictions. Because of the complex issues involved, any claims can require an extended period
to resolve. In management&#146;s opinion, adequate provisions for income taxes have been made.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica records a valuation allowance to reduce its deferred tax assets to the amount that
Applica believes will more likely than not be realized. While Applica considers future taxable
income and ongoing prudent and feasible tax planning strategies in assessing the need for the
valuation allowance, in the event it was to determine that it would not be able to realize all or
part of its net deferred tax assets in the future, an adjustment to the deferred tax assets would
be charged to tax expense in the period such determination is made. Likewise, should Applica
determine that it would be able to realize its deferred tax assets in the future in excess of its
net recorded amount, an adjustment to the deferred tax assets would increase net income in the
period such determination is made.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<U><B><I>Collectibility of Accounts Receivable</I></B></U>. Applica records allowances for estimated
losses resulting from the inability of its customers to make required payments on their balances.
Applica assesses the credit worthiness of its customers based on multiple sources of information
and analyzes factors including:
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt">
<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="font-size: 10pt">

<TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="5%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>Applica&#146;s historical bad debt experiences;</TD>
</TR>

<TR>
    <TD style="font-size: 6pt">&nbsp;</TD>
</TR><TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="5%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>publicly available information regarding its customers and the inherent credit
risk related to them;</TD>
</TR>

<TR>
    <TD style="font-size: 6pt">&nbsp;</TD>
</TR><TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="5%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>information from subscription-based credit reporting companies;</TD>
</TR>

<TR>
    <TD style="font-size: 6pt">&nbsp;</TD>
</TR><TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="5%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>trade association data and reports;</TD>
</TR>

<TR>
    <TD style="font-size: 6pt">&nbsp;</TD>
</TR><TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="5%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>current economic trends; and</TD>
</TR>

<TR>
    <TD style="font-size: 6pt">&nbsp;</TD>
</TR><TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="5%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>changes in customer payment terms or payment patterns.</TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;This assessment requires significant judgment. If the financial condition of Applica&#146;s
customers were to worsen, additional write-offs may be required. Such write-offs may not be
included in the allowance for doubtful accounts at December&nbsp;31, 2005 and, therefore, a charge to
income could result in the period in which a particular customer&#146;s financial condition worsens.
Conversely, if the financial condition of Applica&#146;s customers were to improve or its judgment
regarding their financial condition was to change positively, a reduction in the allowances may be
required resulting in an increase in income in the period such determination is made. Applica
currently has a credit approved receivables purchasing agreement with FCIA Underwriters. The
agreement allows Applica to
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">-24-
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">transfer to FCIA, without recourse, up to 90% of approved receivables of specified customers under
certain circumstances, including the bankruptcy of covered customers, up to a maximum aggregate
amount of $10&nbsp;million
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<U><B><I>Inventory</I></B></U>. Applica values inventory at the lower of cost or market, using the
first-in, first-out (FIFO)&nbsp;method, and regularly reviews the book value of discontinued product
lines and stock keeping units (SKUs) to determine if these items are properly valued. If market
value is less than cost, Applica writes down the related inventory to the estimated net realizable
value. Applica regularly evaluates the composition of inventory to identify slow-moving and
obsolete inventories to determine if additional write-downs are required. This valuation requires
significant judgment from management as to the salability of its inventory based on forecasted
sales. It is particularly difficult to judge the potential sales of new products. Should the
forecasted sales not materialize, it would have a significant impact on Applica&#146;s results of
operations and the valuation of its inventory, resulting in a charge to income in the period such
determination is made. Applica values its inventory in Mexico at average cost.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<U><B><I>Product Liability Claims and Litigation</I></B></U>. Applica is subject to lawsuits and other
claims related to product and other matters that are being defended and handled in the ordinary
course of business. Applica maintains accruals for such costs that may be incurred, which are
determined on a case-by-case basis, taking into consideration the likelihood of adverse judgments
or outcomes, as well as the potential range of probable loss. The accruals are monitored on an
ongoing basis and are updated for new developments or new information as appropriate. With respect
to product liability claims, Applica estimates the amount of ultimate liability in excess of
applicable insurance coverage based on historical claims experience and current claim estimates, as
well as other available facts and circumstances.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Management believes that the amount of ultimate liability of Applica&#146;s current claims and
litigation matters, if any, is not likely to have a material effect on its business, financial
condition, results of operations or liquidity. However, as the outcome of litigation is difficult
to predict, unfavorable significant changes in the estimated exposures could occur resulting in a
charge to income in the period such determination is made. Conversely, if favorable changes in the
estimated exposures occur, a reduction in the accruals may be required resulting in an increase in
income in the period such determination is made.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<U><B><I>Long-Lived Assets</I></B></U>. Applica reviews long-lived assets and certain identifiable
intangibles held and used for possible impairment whenever events or changes in circumstances
indicate that the carrying amount of an asset may not be recoverable. In evaluating the fair value
and future benefits of its intangible assets, management performs an analysis of the anticipated
undiscounted future net cash flows of the individual assets over the remaining amortization period.
Applica recognizes an impairment loss if the carrying value of the asset exceeds the expected
future cash flows.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<U><B><I>Other Estimates</I></B></U>. During the years, Applica has made significant estimates in
connection with specific events affecting its expectations. These have included accruals relating
to the consolidation of its operations, plant closings, reduction in employees and product recalls.
Estimates have also been required with respect to Applica&#146;s investment in a joint venture
investment partnership, Anasazi Partners, L.P. (For additional information, see Note D, Equity In
Net Earnings of Joint Venture and Other Income of the Notes, to Consolidated Financial Statements
included in Schedule&nbsp;I of this Annual Report on Form 10-K.) Applica makes a number of other
estimates in the ordinary course of business relating to sales returns and allowances, warranty
accruals, and accruals for promotional incentives. Historically, past changes to these estimates
have not had a material impact on Applica&#146;s financial condition but have significantly affected
operations from time to time. However, circumstances could change which may alter future
expectations.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">-25-
</DIV>

    <TD>Effective January&nbsp;1, 2005, Applica modified its accounting treatment for cooperative
advertising and slotting fees. This modification resulted in a reclassification that
reduced each of net sales, gross profit and selling, general and administrative expenses by
2.4% in 2005, 2.3% in 2004 and 2.7% in 2003. The reclassification has been reflected in
the percentages presented above. Because the modification resulted solely in a
reclassification within the consolidated statement of operations, there was no impact on
Applica&#146;s financial condition, operating income or net earnings for any periods presented.</TD>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Year Ended December&nbsp;31, 2005 Compared to Year Ended December&nbsp;31, 2004</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Net Sales</I></B>. Consolidated net sales decreased by $153.7&nbsp;million to $556.1&nbsp;million, a decrease
of 21.6% over 2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Sales for the Household Product segment, net of inter-segment sales, for the year ended
December&nbsp;31, 2005 decreased from $627.2&nbsp;million to $492.4&nbsp;million. For the year ended December&nbsp;31,
2005:
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt">
<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="font-size: 10pt">

    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>sales of Black &#038;
Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP>
branded products decreased by $126.7&nbsp;million to $432.0&nbsp;million;</TD>
</TR>

    <TD style="font-size: 6pt">&nbsp;</TD>

    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>sales of Littermaid<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> branded products increased by $1.8&nbsp;million to $37.9&nbsp;million; and</TD>

</TABLE>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In 2005, sales of Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> branded products decreased primarily as the result of (1)
the elimination of certain products identified in our product and customer profitability review (2)
lower sales of promotional items during the Holiday period (3)&nbsp;lower sales of the Home Cafe&#153; single
cup coffee makers and (4)&nbsp;inventory management by significant customers.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Sales for the Professional Personal Care segment for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2005
decreased from $64.9&nbsp;million to $54.8&nbsp;million for the 2004 period. This decrease was primarily the
result of the sale of the Jerdon hotel and hospitality business in October&nbsp;2004. Sales of products by the Jerdon division
totaled $8.4&nbsp;million in 2004.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">-26-
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Sales for the Manufacturing segment for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2005 decreased from $191.4
million to $53.5&nbsp;million for the 2004 period. In 2005, the intersegment sales decreased from
$173.8&nbsp;million to $44.6&nbsp;million. In 2005, contract manufacturing decreased from $17.7&nbsp;million to
$8.9&nbsp;million, primarily as the result of the sale of our Hong Kong-based manufacturing operations
in July&nbsp;2004. Contract manufacturing sales from our Hong Kong manufacturing operations were $14.2
million in 2004. The decrease was offset by an increase in contract manufacturing at our
manufacturing operations in Mexico from $3.5&nbsp;million to $8.9&nbsp;million, primarily related to one
project. The manufacturing facility in Mexico ceased operations in October&nbsp;2005.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Restructuring Charges. </I></B>In 2005, Applica incurred restructuring charges of $12.5&nbsp;million
relating to the continued downsizing in the first half of 2005 and ultimate closure of its Mexican
manufacturing operations in October&nbsp;2005. In February&nbsp;2006, Applica entered into a sale agreement
with an unrelated third party to sell the building housing the factory in Queretaro, Mexico for
$5.3&nbsp;million (net of commissions). Applica expects the sale to close in the second quarter of
2006.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In 2004, Applica incurred restructuring charges of $9.2&nbsp;million, primarily relating to the
downsizing of its Mexican manufacturing operations.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Product Recall Expenses. </I></B>In March&nbsp;2005, Applica voluntarily recalled approximately 500,000
Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> branded BL 5000, BL 5900 and BL 6000 blenders. Substantially all costs and
expenses related to this recall were reimbursed in 2005 by the supplier who manufactured the
blenders.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Gross Profit</I></B>. Applica&#146;s gross profit decreased by $69.7&nbsp;million to $124.2&nbsp;million in 2005
primarily as the result of (1)&nbsp;lower sales volume, (2)&nbsp;restructuring charges and losses at our
Mexican manufacturing operations, (3)&nbsp;inventory write-downs and (4)&nbsp;higher warranty and related
expenses. Applica&#146;s gross profit margin as a percentage of sales decreased to 22.3% for the year
ended December&nbsp;31, 2005 as compared to 27.3% for 2004. The gross profit margin decrease was
primarily attributed to:
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt">
<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="font-size: 10pt">

<TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="5%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>inventory write-downs of $12.8&nbsp;million related to adjustment to net realizable
value of the Home Cafe&#153; single cup coffee maker and the Tide&#153; Buzz&#153; ultrasonic
stain remover;</TD>
</TR>

<TR>
    <TD style="font-size: 6pt">&nbsp;</TD>
</TR><TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="5%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>higher product warranty returns and related expenses of $5.2&nbsp;million primarily
in the first half of 2005; and</TD>
</TR>

<TR>
    <TD style="font-size: 6pt">&nbsp;</TD>
</TR><TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="5%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>restructuring charges of $12.5&nbsp;million in 2005 related to the downsizing and
ultimate closure of our Mexican manufacturing operations, as compared to
restructuring charges of $9.2&nbsp;million in 2004, primarily related to the downsizing
of our Mexican manufacturing operations.</TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;We also experienced higher unabsorbed overhead and inefficiencies of $7.1&nbsp;million at our
Mexican manufacturing operations in 2005 as the result of reduced production associated with the
downsizing and closure activities of such operations, which commenced in the fourth quarter of
2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;At December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004, we had $2.9&nbsp;million and $2.0&nbsp;million, respectively, of
capitalized manufacturing costs associated with the higher unabsorbed overhead and inefficiencies
at our Mexican manufacturing operations. Included in cost of goods sold for 2005 were $6.2&nbsp;million
of these costs. We expect to sell through the entire inventory manufactured at our Mexican
facilities on hand at December&nbsp;31, 2005 by April&nbsp;2006. We also expect to incur administrative
costs of approximately $0.5&nbsp;million associated with the closure of our Mexico Manufacturing
operations through April&nbsp;2006.
</DIV>



<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Additionally, in 2005, we recorded $0.7&nbsp;million in allowances for doubtful accounts related to
a contract manufacturing project manufactured at our Mexican manufacturing operations. This
charge was included as a component of cost of goods sold in the accompanying consolidated statement
of operations.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In the first half of 2005, sales of the first generation Home Caf&#233;&#153; and Tide&#153; Buzz&#153; were lower
than we had anticipated. The size of the Tide&#153; Buzz&#153; product, the relative complexity of use and
price were the main reasons given by consumers for not purchasing the product. Based on this
information, we decided to close out the first generation of the Tide&#153; Buzz&#153; in the first quarter
of 2005 and took steps to accelerate the introduction of the next generation. In the second
quarter of 2005, our alliance partner introduced a product that performed relatively
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">-27-
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">the same function as the next generation of the Tide&#153; Buzz&#153; at a price point that made it
impractical for us to continue with the development of the next generation, which was terminated in
the second quarter of 2005.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Our Home Caf&#233;&#153; sales plan for 2005 was based on promotional campaigns that did not fully
materialize. This resulted in lower-than-anticipated consumer demand for the Home Caf&#233;&#153; coffee
maker and excess inventory of such product. As a result, in the first quarter of 2005, we wrote
down the first generation of Home Caf&#233;&#153; inventory to its net realizable value based on facts and
circumstances existing at the time. In the second quarter, we revised the net realizable value of
the Home Caf&#233;&#153; inventory primarily based on a lower than anticipated selling price.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In 2005, we experienced an increase in our warranty returns and related expenses, primarily in
the first half of 2005. We believe that we have taken appropriate measures to combat this issue in
a timely and effective manner. These measures include the retention of an independent third party
quality consultant to oversee the production process at our major suppliers in China.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Additionally, 2005 was negatively impacted by inflation pressures on the price of raw
materials and increases in oil prices.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The decreases in gross profit margins were partially offset by improved product mix, primarily
as a result of the elimination of certain products identified in our product and customer
profitability review and the movement of the manufacturing of most all of our products to China.
We expect to see the benefits of the decision to close our Mexican manufacturing facility in 2006
as we continue to work with our suppliers in China to obtain the lowest possible product cost
without compromising quality. However, we do not expect to fully realize gross margin improvements
from moving production to the Far East until all of the remaining products manufactured by our
Mexican facility are sold through to our customers.
</DIV>

$0.8&nbsp;million, primarily related to the realization of cumulative foreign currency translation
adjustments. In the fourth quarter of 2004, we sold the Jerdon hotel and hospitality division and
recorded a gain on sale of approximately $3.4&nbsp;million. Also in the fourth quarter of 2004, we sold
our executive offices located in Miami Lakes, Florida, which resulted in a gain of approximately
$1.3&nbsp;million.

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<U><B>Impairment of Goodwill</B></U><I>. </I>As of June&nbsp;30, 2004, Applica performed its annual fair value
assessment of goodwill, with the assistance of an independent third party valuation group, and
determined that the implied value of Applica&#146;s goodwill was zero, resulting in a non-cash

as a component of selling, general and administrative expenses in the consolidated statement of
operations of 2004.

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Interest Expense</I></B>. Interest expense increased by $1.6&nbsp;million, or 16.6%, to $11.4&nbsp;million in
2005 as compared to $9.8&nbsp;million in 2004 as the result of higher interest rates.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Interest and Other Income</I></B><I>. </I>In July&nbsp;2003, ZonePerfect Nutrition Company, an investment held by
a partnership that was 50% owned by Applica, was sold for approximately $160.0&nbsp;million. The
partnership was dissolved in the third quarter of 2004. A portion of the proceeds from the sale of
ZonePerfect was being held in escrow as of December&nbsp;31, 2004, $8.4&nbsp;million of which was owed to
Applica. Half of this amount ($4.2&nbsp;million) was recorded as part of the equity in net earnings of
joint ventures in 2003. At December&nbsp;31, 2004, Applica had not collected any portion of the
escrowed funds and had included the $4.2&nbsp;million in other receivables. Management believed that
the collection of the remaining $4.2&nbsp;million was uncertain and, therefore, such amount was not
recorded into income as of December&nbsp;31, 2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In February&nbsp;2005, Applica received approximately $1.6&nbsp;million in the first distribution of the
funds held in escrow. In August&nbsp;2005, Applica received approximately $3.4&nbsp;million in the second
distribution of the escrowed funds. Applica applied these receipts, totaling $5.0&nbsp;million, to the
receivable balance at December&nbsp;31, 2004 of $4.2&nbsp;million and recorded the income of $0.8&nbsp;million as
other income for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2005.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The remainder of the funds in escrow are subject to dispute and, in December&nbsp;2005, a lawsuit
was filed against the purchaser of ZonePerfect claiming that there is no basis to withhold the
distribution of the remaining funds. If and when the claims made on the remaining escrowed funds
are resolved in its favor, Applica could receive cash and record additional other income of up to
$3.4&nbsp;million, although it is likely that the claims will be settled for less.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica&#146;s equity in the net earnings of joint venture was zero for the years ended December
31, 2005 and 2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>(Gain) Loss On Early Extinguishment of Debt</I></B>. In October&nbsp;2005, Applica entered into a secured
term loan agreement with Mast Credit Opportunities I, (Master) Ltd. to borrow $20&nbsp;million.
Applica used a portion of the proceeds from the term loan to repurchase from Mast $5.0&nbsp;million of
its 10% senior subordinated notes due 2008 at 98% of par value. The repurchase of the bonds
resulted in a gain on redemption of approximately $0.06&nbsp;million, net of the pro-rata write-off of
deferred financing costs related to the redemption. The balance of the proceeds was used to pay
down Applica&#146;s senior revolving credit facility.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In February&nbsp;2004, Applica redeemed $4.25&nbsp;million of the 10% notes. The notes were redeemed at
prices between 103.25% and 103.33% of the principal amount, plus accrued interest. The cost of the
redemption included $0.2&nbsp;million in prepayment premiums and the pro-rata write-off of deferred
financing costs related to the redemption.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Vendor Consideration. </I></B>Applica&#146;s supplier of vacuum packaging products agreed to reimburse it
$4.0&nbsp;million for a portion of the costs of litigating a patent infringement matter with Tilia
International, Inc. The reimbursement is evidenced by an unsecured note receivable bearing
interest at 6% per annum and is payable in equal quarterly installments of $200,000, plus interest,
over five years. We believe that the ultimate collection of the note receivable is not assured and
is dependent on Applica&#146;s future purchases from the supplier. Therefore, the amount collected on
the note receivable is recorded on the &#147;cash basis&#148; over the five-year period as a reduction of
future product costs. Applica collected cash of $228,000 and $380,000 from the supplier in 2005
and 2004, respectively, and recorded it as a reduction of cost of goods sold in each such year. We
do not expect future purchases from the supplier to be significant and, therefore, the
collectibility of the note receivable is highly unlikely.
</DIV>

<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Taxes. </I></B>Applica&#146;s tax provision is based on an estimated annual aggregation of the taxes on
earnings of each of its foreign and domestic operations. For 2005, Applica incurred a tax expense
of $2.9&nbsp;million most of which was attributed to foreign operations and additional valuation
allowances on deferred tax assets. For 2004, Applica incurred a tax expense of $59.5&nbsp;million,
which included the impact of an impairment of goodwill, a provision for previously untaxed foreign
earnings, and additional valuation allowances on deferred tax assets.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;SFAS No.&nbsp;109, &#147;Accounting for Income Taxes&#148; requires that a valuation allowance be established
when it is more likely than not that all or a portion of a deferred tax asset will not be realized.
A review of all available positive and negative evidence needs to be considered, including a
company&#146;s current and past performance, the market environment in which the company operates, the
utilization of past tax credits, length of carryback and carryforward periods. Forming a
conclusion that a valuation allowance is not needed is difficult when there is negative objective
evidence such as cumulative losses in recent years. Cumulative losses weigh heavily in the overall
assessment. As a result of its reviews, Applica concluded that it was appropriate to record
additional valuation allowances of $18.1&nbsp;million in 2005. Applica expects to realize the benefits
of the remaining net deferred tax assets of approximately $10.4&nbsp;million as of December&nbsp;31, 2005,
primarily from identified tax planning strategies.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;We expect to continue to maintain a valuation allowance on certain future tax benefits until
an appropriate level of profitability is reached or we are able to develop tax strategies which
would enable us to conclude that it is more likely than not that a portion of our net deferred tax
assets would be realized.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;For additional information regarding taxes, see Note N, Income Taxes, of the Notes to
Consolidated Financial Statements included in Schedule&nbsp;I of this Annual Report on Form 10-K.
</DIV>

were 24,150,991 and 23,974,664, respectively. All common stock equivalents have been excluded from
the diluted per share calculations in 2005 and 2004 because their inclusion would have been
anti-dilutive. Potential common stock equivalents at
December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004 were 2,483,224 and
2,755,207 with exercise prices ranging from $1.62 to $31.69 per share and $3.63 and $31.69 per
share, respectively.

claims continued to diminish considerably. In the fourth quarter of 2003, management determined
that an accrual related to the product recall was no longer required and $4.1&nbsp;million of such
accrual was reversed and was included as a reduction of cost of sales.

    <TD width="5%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>lower amortization of intangible assets of $7.1&nbsp;million, primarily related to
the write-off of the unamortized book value of an intangible asset related to the
Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> tradename in the fourth quarter of 2003; and</TD>
</TR>

<TR>
    <TD style="font-size: 6pt">&nbsp;</TD>
</TR><TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="5%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>a decrease in legal expenses of $3.2&nbsp;million, primarily as the result of the
significant legal expenses related to the Tilia litigation, which was settled in
2004.</TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Operating expenses decreased as a percentage of sales to 26.9% in 2004 from 27.2% in the 2003
period primarily as the result of higher sales volume in 2004 leveraging fixed costs.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<U><B>Termination Benefits.</B></U> In the third quarter of 2004, Applica incurred termination
costs of approximately $9.2&nbsp;million related to the resignation of Applica&#146;s former Chairman of the
Board and the termination of certain other employment and consulting agreements and relationships.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<U><B>Gain on the Sale of Subsidiary, Division and Property.</B></U> In the third quarter of 2004,
we sold our Hong Kong manufacturing subsidiary, and recorded a loss on the sale of approximately
$0.8&nbsp;million, primarily from the realization of cumulative foreign currency translation
adjustments. In the fourth quarter of 2004, we sold the Jerdon hotel and hospitality division and
recorded a gain on sale of approximately $3.4&nbsp;million. Also in the fourth quarter, we sold our
executive offices located in Miami Lakes, Florida, which resulted in a gain of approximately $1.3
million.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<U><B>Restructuring and Other (Credits) Charges</B></U>. In the first quarter of 2004, we settled
an outstanding litigation matter for $125,000 and reversed the remaining accrual of $563,000
related to such litigation. In 2003, Applica incurred additional expenses of $4.7&nbsp;million relating
to its previous decision to consolidate its Shelton, Connecticut office with the headquarters then
located in Miami Lakes, Florida. Applica was unable to rent a majority of the facility for the
remainder of its lease as originally planned.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<U><B>Impairment of Goodwill</B></U><I>. </I>As of June&nbsp;30, 2004, we performed our annual fair value
assessment of goodwill, with the assistance of an independent third party valuation group, and
determined that the implied value of Applica&#146;s goodwill was zero, resulting in a non-cash
adjustment in the carrying value of goodwill of $62.8&nbsp;million. The impairment charge was included
as a component of selling, general and administrative expenses in the consolidated statement of
operations of 2004.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In accordance with SFAS No.&nbsp;142 &#147;Goodwill and Other Intangible Assets,&#148; the testing for
impairment of goodwill is performed in two steps: (1)&nbsp;potential impairment is identified by
comparing the fair value of a reporting unit with its carrying amount (including goodwill); and (2)
if fair value is less than the carrying amount, an impairment loss is estimated as the excess of
the carrying amount of the goodwill over its implied value.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The impairment of goodwill was primarily due to lower than expected cash flows, resulting from
lower-than-anticipated gross profit margins attributable to increased costs of raw materials and
higher transportation costs, which are expected to continue for the foreseeable future. The
implied fair value of goodwill was estimated using a combination of market multiples, comparable
transactions and discounted cash flow methodologies.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The implied fair value of goodwill was determined by allocating Applica&#146;s fair value to all of
Applica&#146;s assets and liabilities. Any excess of fair value over the amount allocated represented
implied fair value of goodwill. The allocation process was performed solely for purposes of testing
goodwill for impairment. Thus, the carrying amounts of assets and liabilities, other than goodwill,
were not affected.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The fair value of the individual assets is the amount at which an asset could be bought or
sold in a current transaction not involving a forced or liquidating sale. The fair value of the
individual liabilities is the amount at which a liability could be incurred or settled in a current
transaction not involving a forced or liquidating sale.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">-32-
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In our process of allocating the fair value to Applica&#146;s tangible assets, primarily accounts
receivable, inventory and manufacturing property, plant and equipment in Mexico, and identifiable
intangible assets (i.e., customer relationships and tradenames) and liabilities, we determined that
the implied value of goodwill (the residual) was zero.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<U><B>Impairment of Intangible Asset</B></U><B><I>. </I></B>In June&nbsp;1998, Applica acquired the household products
group of The Black &#038; Decker Corporation for $319.8&nbsp;million in cash and assumed certain related
liabilities. As part of the acquisition, Applica acquired the right to use the Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP>
trademark in four product categories: garment care, cooking, food preparation and beverage.
Applica has the right to sell products under this license agreement in North America, Central
America, and South America, excluding Brazil. The excess of the purchase price over the estimated
fair value of the acquired net assets was $228.8&nbsp;million, of which $47.2&nbsp;million was allocated to
an intangible asset related to the Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> trademark and the remaining $181.6&nbsp;million was
allocated to goodwill. The $47.2&nbsp;million assigned to the fair value of the intangible asset was
based on a valuation analysis performed by Applica as of June&nbsp;1998. For the first five years
(through June&nbsp;30, 2003), the license was on a royalty free basis for the core product categories.
Mutually agreed upon renewals were to be at the stated royalty rate, along with specified minimum
royalty payments. The agreement provided for an initial period of five years, with a subsequent
five-year extended term and up to three additional five-year extensions.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Beginning in 2002, sales of Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> branded products under the original four product
categories began to decrease significantly, which decrease continued through December&nbsp;31, 2003.
Sales of Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> branded products under the original four product categories for 2003,
2002, and 2001 were $399&nbsp;million, $429&nbsp;million and $446&nbsp;million, respectively. As a result of the
continuation of the lower sales of Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> branded products in the four core product
categories in the third and fourth quarter of 2003 (which are Applica&#146;s busiest quarters due to the
seasonality of our business), management determined that a triggering event had occurred in
accordance with SFAS 144, &#147;Accounting for the Impairment or Disposal of Long-Lived Assets&#148;.
Accordingly, Applica performed a valuation analysis by calculating the expected effective royalty
rate in 2004 through 2006, taking into account the minimum royalties, as well as the projected
sales of Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> branded products under the original four categories. Based on the
valuation analysis, management believed that there was no remaining value in the initial intangible
asset as of December&nbsp;31, 2003. Accordingly, Applica recorded an impairment charge of approximately
$7.2&nbsp;million to write off the unamortized book value of the intangible asset. The impairment charge
was reflected in the fourth quarter of 2003 because the decreased fourth quarter sales provided
further confirmation of an impairment. Pursuant to the requirements of SFAS 144 and as a result of
Applica&#146;s valuation analysis, the impairment charge was classified as a component of income from
operations.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Interest Expense</I></B>. Interest expense decreased by $4.2&nbsp;million, or 29.8%, to $9.8&nbsp;million in
2004 as compared to $14.0&nbsp;million in 2003 as the result of lower debt levels and lower average
interest rates. The lower debt levels resulted from the redemption of $65.0&nbsp;million of our 10%
Senior Subordinated Notes due 2008 during the second half of 2003 and the redemption of $4.25
million in February&nbsp;2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Loss On Early Extinguishment of Debt</I></B>. In February&nbsp;2004, Applica redeemed $4.25&nbsp;million of its
10% notes. The notes were redeemed at prices between 103.25% and 103.33% of the principal amount,
plus accrued interest. The cost of the redemption included $187,000 in prepayment premiums and the
pro-rata write-off of deferred financing costs related to the redemption. In July, October and
November&nbsp;2003, Applica redeemed a total of $65&nbsp;million of the notes. The notes were redeemed at
prices between 103% and 105% of the principal amount, plus accrued interest. The cost of the 2003
redemption included $2.8&nbsp;million in prepayment premiums and $1.1&nbsp;million related to the pro-rata
write-off of deferred financing costs.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Equity in Net Earnings of Joint Venture</I></B><B>. </B>In July&nbsp;2003, ZonePerfect Nutrition Company, an
investment held by a partnership that was 50% owned by Applica, was sold for approximately $160.0
million. Such sale resulted in an increase in net earnings of joint ventures of $55.6&nbsp;million in
2003.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;A portion of the sale price was being held in escrow as of December&nbsp;31, 2004 and 2003, $8.4
million of which is owed to Applica. Half of this amount ($4.2&nbsp;million) was recorded as part of
the increase in net earnings of joint ventures in 2003. Management believed that the collection of
the remaining $4.2&nbsp;million was uncertain and, therefore, such amount was not recorded into income
as of December&nbsp;31, 2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The partnership was dissolved in the third quarter of 2004.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">-33-
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Vendor Consideration. </I></B>Applica&#146;s supplier of vacuum packaging products agreed to reimburse us
$4.0&nbsp;million for a portion of the costs of litigating a patent infringement matter with Tilia
International, Inc. The reimbursement was evidenced by an unsecured note receivable bearing
interest at 6% per annum and is payable in equal quarterly installments of $200,000, plus interest,
over five years. We believe that the ultimate collection of the note receivable is not assured and
is dependent on Applica&#146;s future purchases from the supplier. Therefore, the amount collected on
the note receivable is recorded on the &#147;cash basis&#148; over the five-year period as a reduction of
future product costs. Applica collected cash of $380,000 from the supplier in 2004 and recorded
it as a reduction of cost of goods sold.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Taxes</I></B>. Applica&#146;s tax provision is based on an estimated annual aggregation of the taxes on
earnings of each of its foreign and domestic operations. For 2004, Applica incurred a tax expense
of $59.5&nbsp;million, which included the impact of an impairment of goodwill, a provision for
previously untaxed foreign earnings, and additional valuation allowances on deferred tax assets.
For 2003, Applica incurred a tax expense of $10.1&nbsp;million.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In the second quarter of 2004, Applica changed its position regarding the permanent investment
of certain foreign undistributed earnings, primarily as a result of its decision to exit its Hong
Kong-based manufacturing operations. In the fourth quarter of 2004, Applica repatriated $78.4
million of the $85.5&nbsp;million of the previously untaxed earnings for which a related tax expense of
$24.0&nbsp;million was recorded in the second quarter of 2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;SFAS No.&nbsp;109, &#147;Accounting for Income Taxes&#148; requires that a valuation allowance be established
when it is more likely than not that all or a portion of a deferred tax asset will not be realized.
A review of all available positive and negative evidence needs to be considered, including a
company&#146;s current and past performance, the market environment in which the company operates, the
utilization of past tax credits, length of carryback and carryforward periods. Forming a
conclusion that a valuation allowance is not needed is difficult when there is negative objective
evidence such as cumulative losses in recent years. Cumulative losses weigh heavily in the overall
assessment. As a result of its quarterly reviews, Applica concluded that it was appropriate to
record additional valuation allowances of $75.2&nbsp;million in 2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;For additional information regarding taxes, see Note N, Income Taxes, of the Notes to
Consolidated Financial Statements included in Schedule&nbsp;I of this Annual Report on Form 10-K.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Earnings Per Share</I></B>. Weighted average basic shares for the periods ended December&nbsp;31, 2004 and
2003 were 23,974,664 and 23,572,857, respectively. Included in diluted shares is the dilutive
effect of common stock equivalents relating to stock options of 424,703 for the year ended December
31, 2003. All common stock equivalents have been excluded from the diluted per share calculations
in 2004 because their inclusion would have been anti-dilutive. Potential common stock equivalents
at December&nbsp;31, 2004 and 2003 were 2,755,207 and 1,494,685 with exercise prices ranging from $3.63
to $31.69 per share and $6.75 and $31.69 per share, respectively.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Our material short-term cash requirements are the funds necessary to maintain current

receivable and paying operating expenses, including Black and Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> royalty payments, lease
payments and interest costs. We expect our operating expenses to remain similar to historical
percentages of sales. Our interest costs will fluctuate based upon interest rates, as well as our
ability to generate cash flow to pay down debt. The table below provides a summary of other future
expected cash obligations. In addition, we require funds for capital expenditures for tooling for
new products, information technology improvements and other improvements.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In February&nbsp;2006, we announced that we initiated an external process to explore strategic
alternatives to enhance shareholder value, which could include a sale or merger of Applica. In
connection therewith, Applica adopted an employee retention plan, which classifies employees into
different bands based on their level of responsibility and role within the company. The total
amount of bonuses that could be paid under the plan is approximately $1.5&nbsp;million and is based on
an employee&#146;s base salary at the time the bonus is determined. The bonus is payable upon the
earlier of the close of a transaction or December&nbsp;31, 2006, and is contingent upon the continued
employment of the employee through the payment date. The above retention plan does not cover
individuals with employment contracts.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Effective December&nbsp;1, 2005, our accounts payable terms with Elec-Tech changed from 60&nbsp;days
form invoice date to 30&nbsp;days from invoice date.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Historically, we have financed our short-term cash requirements primarily through cash flows
from operations, borrowings under our senior credit facility, other short-term borrowings, and the
sale of certain assets. We expect to continue to do the same in the future.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Capital expenditures are expected to be approximately $4.0&nbsp;million in 2006 compared to $3.4
million in 2005.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Our material long-term cash requirements consist mainly of the $55.8&nbsp;million of our 10% notes,
which are due in July&nbsp;2008, and the $20&nbsp;million term loan due November&nbsp;2009. In addition, our
ongoing future cash requirements include future operating expenses, payments under our Black and
Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> trademark license, capital expenditures, interest expense, lease payments and payments
under our senior credit facility.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;We expect to have sufficient liquidity from cash flow from operations and borrowings under our
credit facility to finance our cash requirements. If necessary, we believe we will have access to
the capital markets to fund any unexpected long-term cash requirements.
</DIV>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Sources and Uses of Cash</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Operating Activities. </I></B>In 2005, Applica&#146;s operations used $4.0&nbsp;million in cash, compared with
the use of cash of $63.6&nbsp;million in 2004. The improvement in operating cash flows as compared to
the prior year was principally due to lower working capital requirements as the result of lower
sales in 2005 and lower inventory levels as compared to 2004. Inventory was higher during 2004 as
we built transitional inventory to support the sale of our Hong Kong-based manufacturing operations
and the transition of production of certain products from Mexico to China.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Investing Activities</I></B>. For the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2005, investing activities generated
cash of $6.7&nbsp;million, compared with $34.1&nbsp;million of cash generated in 2004. During 2004, we
received approximately $47.4&nbsp;million from the sale of assets, in addition to $1.2&nbsp;million from
joint venture distributions. Additionally, during 2005, capital expenditures were lower by $11.2
million compared to 2004 primarily due to capital spending of $8.4&nbsp;million in 2004 on our
implementation of a new ERP system as compared to $0.3&nbsp;million in 2005. The implementation was
completed in March&nbsp;2005. Additionally, capital expenditures were lower in 2005 compared to 2004 as
the result of the sale of our Hong Kong-based manufacturing operations in July&nbsp;2004 and the
downsizing and ultimate closure in 2005 of our Mexican manufacturing operations.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In 2006, Applica expects capital expenditures of $4.0&nbsp;million, primarily for new product
development. Applica plans to fund these capital expenditures from cash flow from operations and,
if necessary, borrowings under its credit facility.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Financing Activities</I></B>. Net cash used in financing activities was $8.0&nbsp;million in 2005,
compared to cash provided of $26.5&nbsp;million in 2004. Borrowings under Applica&#146;s lines of credit in
2005 were lower than 2004, primarily attributable to lower working capital requirements.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Debt Instruments, Guarantees and Related Covenants</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica amended and restated its senior credit facility in December&nbsp;2005. The revised $125
million asset-based senior secured revolving credit facility matures in November&nbsp;2009. Advances
under the facility are governed by Applica&#146;s collateral value, which is based primarily on
percentages of outstanding eligible accounts receivable and inventories. Other changes to the
amended credit facility include a reduction in the daily availability and average monthly
availability requirements, and applicable margins based on those averages, and the addition of
receivables collected by our Hong Kong subsidiary to the borrowing base.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Under the amended agreement, if Applica does not maintain a minimum fixed charge coverage
ratio of 1.0 to 1.0, Applica must maintain a minimum daily availability under its borrowing base of
$10&nbsp;million and a minimum average monthly availability of $13&nbsp;million. As of December&nbsp;31, 2005,
Applica&#146;s fixed charge coverage ratio was less than 1.0 to 1.0, but its average monthly
availability in December&nbsp;2005 was $42.1&nbsp;million. If Applica maintains a fixed charge coverage ratio
of greater than 1.0 to 1.0, there is no availability requirement and no availability block.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As of December&nbsp;31, 2005, Applica was borrowing approximately $69.5&nbsp;million under the facility
and had approximately $29.7&nbsp;million available for future cash borrowings, which is net of the $10
million daily block. As of March&nbsp;10, 2006, Applica was borrowing approximately $46.4&nbsp;million under
the facility and had approximately $22.0&nbsp;million available for future cash borrowings, which is net
of the $10&nbsp;million daily block.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The credit facility includes a $10.0&nbsp;million sub-limit for the issuance of letters of credit,
with approximately $1.7&nbsp;million outstanding under the limit as of December&nbsp;31, 2005. At Applica&#146;s
option, interest accrues on the loans made under the credit facility at either:
