<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 18pt"><B>Applica Incorporated and Subsidiaries<BR>
NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS &#151; CONTINUED</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In the fourth quarter of 2003, Applica initiated a plan to move the production of irons and
toaster ovens for the U.S. marketplace from Mexico to third party suppliers in China in an effort
to lower the costs of these products as manufacturers in China were providing good quality and
design at a cost below Applica&#146;s cost of producing these products in Mexico. In connection with
this restructuring plan, in 2004 and 2003, Applica recorded restructuring charges of $4.7&nbsp;million
and $3.7&nbsp;million, respectively. Such charges consisted of $1.2&nbsp;million and $3.3&nbsp;million of
severance in 2004 and 2003, respectively, and $3.5&nbsp;million and $0.4&nbsp;million of impairment charges
associated with the write-down of machinery and equipment in 2004 and 2003, respectively. The
severance was paid in full by the end of 2004. This restructuring was completed in December&nbsp;2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In the third quarter of 2004, Applica initiated a plan to move the production of the Home
Caf&#233;&#153; single cup coffee maker from Mexico to a third party manufacturer in China. In connection
with this restructuring, Applica recorded an impairment of $3.7&nbsp;million, consisting of impairment
charges of approximately $1.4&nbsp;million for machinery and equipment and approximately $2.3&nbsp;million
for obsolete raw materials inventory, net of approximately $0.7&nbsp;million of estimated realizable
value. The restructuring was completed in February&nbsp;2005.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In the fourth quarter of 2003, Applica initiated a plan to restructure its Hong Kong-based
manufacturing operations to focus primarily on products with proprietary technologies, and to
outsource most of its vertical integration. As a result, in the first quarter of 2004, management
closed one factory totaling approximately 437,000 square feet. In connection with this
restructuring plan, Applica recorded charges of approximately $0.8&nbsp;million and $3.3&nbsp;million in 2004
and 2003, respectively, consisting of impairment charges for the write-off of machinery and
equipment of $2.5&nbsp;million and severance of $1.6&nbsp;million, respectively. The severance was paid in
full in 2004. In July&nbsp;2004, Applica sold its manufacturing operations in China.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In December&nbsp;2005, Applica auctioned a significant portion of the machinery and equipment
located in the Mexican manufacturing facility, along with some office furniture and equipment. As
a result of the auction, Applica realized net cash proceeds of approximately $2.9&nbsp;million. As of
December&nbsp;31, 2005, Applica collected $2.0&nbsp;million of such proceeds. The remaining proceeds of $0.9
million were collected in February&nbsp;2006. The December&nbsp;2005 auction resulted in a small gain.
Applica expects to have another auction in March&nbsp;2006 for the remaining property and equipment
located at the manufacturing facility and expects to realize net proceeds of approximately $0.4
million. Applica does not expect the gain or loss associated with such auction to be significant.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Estimated fair value for machinery and equipment was determined by either appraisals or
discounted cash flows.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;All restructuring charges relate to the Manufacturing reportable segment.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>Product Recall</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In February&nbsp;2002, Applica voluntarily recalled approximately 2.1&nbsp;million toasters. In 2001,
Applica took a charge to cost of sales of $13.4&nbsp;million relating to the estimated expenses of such
recalls. At December&nbsp;31, 2003 and 2002, $9.3&nbsp;million and $8.7&nbsp;million, respectively, had been
charged against the accrual. During 2003, recall claims related to the toaster recall continued to
diminish considerably and in the fourth quarter of 2003, management determined the accrual was no
longer required and the remaining accrual of $4.1&nbsp;million was reversed and was included as a
reduction of cost of sales.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In March&nbsp;2005, Applica voluntarily recalled approximately 500,000 Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> branded BL
5000, BL 5900 and BL 6000 blenders. Substantially all costs and expenses related to this recall
were reimbursed in 2005 by the supplier who manufactured the blenders.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE C &#151; RESTRUCTURING AND OTHER (CREDITS)&nbsp;CHARGES</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In the fourth quarter of 2001, Applica initiated a plan to consolidate its Shelton,
Connecticut office, as well as certain back-office and supply chain functions in Canada and Latin
America, with its executive offices. In 2002, Applica recorded $10.6&nbsp;million in restructuring
charges relating to its decision to consolidate these facilities. The consolidation resulted in a
reduction of approximately 70 employees worldwide and annual savings in payroll and occupancy
expenses. In 2003, Applica recorded an additional $4.7&nbsp;million in restructuring charges related to
its
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">F-19
</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 18pt"><B>Applica Incorporated and Subsidiaries<BR>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; text-indent: 0%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">decision to consolidate the Shelton, Connecticut facilities because it was unable to rent a
majority of the facility for the remainder of the lease as originally planned.</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In the first quarter of 2004, Applica settled an outstanding litigation matter for $0.1
million and reversed the remaining accrual of $0.6&nbsp;million related to such litigation.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Accrued liabilities relating to the restructuring and other charges (credits)&nbsp;were zero and
$2.5&nbsp;million at December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004, respectively. These charges (credits)&nbsp;were reflected
as a component of selling, general, and administrative expenses in the accompanying consolidated
statements of operations. All of these charges (credits)&nbsp;relate to the Household Products business
segment.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE D &#151; EQUITY IN NET EARNINGS OF JOINT VENTURE AND OTHER INCOME</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In July&nbsp;2003, ZonePerfect Nutrition Company, an investment held by a partnership that was 50%
owned by Applica, was sold for approximately $160.0&nbsp;million. A portion of the proceeds from the
sale of ZonePerfect was being held in escrow as of December&nbsp;31, 2004, $8.4&nbsp;million of which was
owed to Applica. Half of this amount ($4.2&nbsp;million) was recorded as part of the equity in net
earnings of joint venture in 2003. At December&nbsp;31, 2004, Applica had not collected any portion of
the escrowed funds and had included the $4.2&nbsp;million in other receivables. Management believed
that the collection of the remaining $4.2&nbsp;million was uncertain and, therefore, such amount was not
recorded into income as of December&nbsp;31, 2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In February&nbsp;2005, Applica received approximately $1.6&nbsp;million in the first distribution of the
funds held in escrow. In August&nbsp;2005, Applica received approximately $3.4&nbsp;million in the second
distribution of the escrowed funds. Applica applied these receipts, totaling $5.0&nbsp;million, to the
receivable balance at December&nbsp;31, 2004 of $4.2&nbsp;million and recorded the income of $0.8&nbsp;million as
other income for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2005.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The remainder of the funds in escrow are subject to dispute and, in December&nbsp;2005, a lawsuit
was filed by the representative for the ZonePerfect shareholders against the purchaser of ZonePerfect claiming that
there is no basis to withhold the distribution of the remaining funds. If and when the claims made
on the remaining escrowed funds are resolved in its favor, Applica could receive cash and record
additional other income of up to $3.4&nbsp;million, although it is likely that the claims will be
settled for less.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The partnership was dissolved in the third quarter of 2004. Applica&#146;s equity in the net
earnings of joint venture was zero for the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004 and $55.6&nbsp;million
for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2003. Applica has no additional commitments or exposure related to
this investment.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Included in Applica&#146;s consolidated retained earnings were earnings related to the joint
venture partnership of approximately $54.1&nbsp;million as of December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE E &#151; SALE OF SUBSIDIARY, DIVISION AND PROPERTY</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>Sale of Subsidiary</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In July&nbsp;2004, Applica sold its Hong Kong manufacturing subsidiary for $28.1&nbsp;million, which
allowed Applica to outsource the manufacturing of a substantial amount of its products in China.
The decision to outsource the manufacturing operations did not materially impact the operations and
cash flows of Applica, except for certain contract manufacturing operations.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;For 2004, revenues and gross profit from Applica&#146;s contract manufacturing operations in China
were $14.2&nbsp;million and $3.5&nbsp;million, respectively. Revenues and gross profit for 2003 were $27.5
million and $2.5&nbsp;million, respectively.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The sale of the Hong Kong-based manufacturing subsidiary resulted in a loss of approximately
$0.8&nbsp;million, primarily from the realization of cumulative foreign currency translation
adjustments.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>Sale of Division</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In October&nbsp;2004, Applica sold the Jerdon hotel and hospitality division. This division of the
professional personal care products segment was sold because it no longer fit in the long-term
strategic plans of Applica. The sale resulted in a gain on sale of $3.4&nbsp;million.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Revenues and pre-tax earnings attributable to the Jerdon division for 2004 were $8.6&nbsp;million
and $0.1&nbsp;million, respectively. Revenues and pre-tax earnings for 2003 were $13.3&nbsp;million and $0.7
million, respectively.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>Sale of Property</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In the fourth quarter of 2004, Applica sold its executive offices located in Miami Lakes,
Florida for $9.3&nbsp;million. The sale resulted in a gain of approximately $1.3&nbsp;million. A portion of
the proceeds from the sale were used to repay the $5.7&nbsp;million outstanding mortgage on the property
and the remainder was used to repay a portion of Applica&#146;s credit facility. In October&nbsp;2004,
Applica moved its executive offices to a leased facility in Miramar, Florida.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE F &#151; TERMINATION BENEFITS</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In August&nbsp;2004, David M. Friedson resigned his position as Chairman of the Board and a
director of Applica. In connection with his resignation, Applica entered into a separation
agreement with Mr.&nbsp;Friedson pursuant to which Mr.&nbsp;Friedson&#146;s employment agreement was terminated
and Applica agreed to pay him $6.5&nbsp;million over the next year. Applica also agreed to provide Mr.
Friedson certain health insurance benefits for a period of 18&nbsp;months and to extend the exercise
period of options to purchase 500,000 shares of the common stock of Applica for an exercise price
of $3.625 per share. The options, which would have expired 90&nbsp;days after the termination of Mr.
Friedson&#146;s employment, expired in December&nbsp;2005. The extension of the options resulted in
compensation expense in 2004 of approximately $0.1&nbsp;million. Mr.&nbsp;Friedson repaid approximately $3.1
million in outstanding loans to Applica in 2005.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Additionally, during the third quarter of 2004, Applica incurred termination costs in
connection with the termination of certain other senior management employment and consulting
agreements and relationships of approximately $2.6&nbsp;million, including certain officers in
Hong Kong.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Accrued liabilities relating to the termination benefits were as follows:
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE G &#151; IMPAIRMENT OF GOODWILL AND OTHER INTANGIBLE ASSETS</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Prior to January&nbsp;1, 2002, intangible assets, consisting primarily of goodwill, were being
amortized on a straight line basis over periods ranging from 2.5 to 40&nbsp;years. In June&nbsp;2001, the
Financial Accounting Standards Board issued SFAS 142, &#147;Goodwill and Other Intangible Assets&#148;, which
established new accounting and reporting requirements for goodwill and other intangible assets.
The new standard required that all intangible assets acquired that are obtained through contractual
or legal right, or are capable of being separately sold, transferred, licensed, rented or
exchanged, must be recognized as an asset apart from goodwill. Goodwill and intangibles with
indefinite lives are no longer amortized, but are subject to an annual assessment for impairment by
applying a fair value based test.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>Goodwill</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica applied the provisions of SFAS 142 beginning on January&nbsp;1, 2002 and performed a
transitional fair valued based impairment test. In accordance with SFAS 142, the testing for
impairment of goodwill is performed in two steps: (1)&nbsp;potential impairment is identified by
comparing the fair value of a reporting unit with its carrying amount (including goodwill); and (2)
if fair value is less than the carrying amount, an impairment loss is estimated as the excess of
the carrying amount of the goodwill over its implied value.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Based on the initial impairment test, performed with the assistance of an independent third
party valuation group, Applica determined that the implied value of its goodwill was $49.5&nbsp;million,
resulting in a non-cash adjustment in the carrying value of goodwill of $121.3&nbsp;million ($78.8
million, net of tax) in the first quarter of 2002. The implied fair value of goodwill was
estimated using a combination of market multiples, comparable transactions and discounted cash flow
methodologies. In accordance with SFAS 142, the impairment charge was reflected as a cumulative
effect of change in accounting principle in the first quarter of 2002.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The implied fair value of goodwill was determined by allocating the fair value of Applica to
all of Applica&#146;s assets and liabilities; any excess of fair value over the amount allocated
represented implied fair value of goodwill. The allocation process was performed solely for
purposes of testing goodwill for impairment. Thus, the carrying amounts of assets and liabilities,
other than goodwill, were not affected.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The fair value of the individual assets is the amount at which an asset could be bought or
sold in a current transaction not involving a forced or liquidating sale. The fair value of the
individual liabilities is the amount at which a liability could be incurred or settled in a current
transaction not involving a forced or liquidating sale.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">F-22
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 18pt"><B>Applica Incorporated and Subsidiaries<BR>
NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS &#151; CONTINUED</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In Applica&#146;s process of allocating the fair value to its tangible assets, primarily accounts
receivable, inventory and manufacturing property, plant and equipment in China and Mexico, and
identifiable intangible assets (i.e., customer relationships and tradenames) and liabilities,
Applica determined that the implied value of goodwill (the residual) was $49.5&nbsp;million.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As of June&nbsp;30, 2002 and 2003, Applica performed its annual fair value assessment, with the
assistance of a third party valuation group, and determined that no additional impairment charge
was necessary. Due to lower than expected sales of certain core categories of Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP>
branded products in the third and fourth quarters of 2003, Applica performed an additional fair
value assessment as of December&nbsp;31, 2003 with the assistance of an independent third party
valuation group, and determined that no impairment charge was necessary.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As of June&nbsp;30, 2004, Applica performed its annual fair value assessment of goodwill, with the
assistance of an independent third party valuation group, and determined that the implied value of
its goodwill was zero, resulting in a non-cash adjustment in the carrying value of goodwill of
$62.8&nbsp;million in the second quarter of 2004. The impairment charge was included as a component of
selling, general and administrative expenses in the accompanying statement of operations for 2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The impairment of goodwill was primarily due to lower than expected cash flows, resulting from
lower-than-anticipated gross profit margins attributable to increased costs of raw materials and
higher transportation costs. Implied fair value of goodwill was estimated using a combination of
market multiples, comparable transactions and discounted cash flow methodologies. The implied fair
value of goodwill at June&nbsp;30, 2004, was determined by allocating the fair value of Applica to all
of Applica&#146;s assets and liabilities; any excess of fair value over the amount allocated represented
implied fair value of goodwill.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In Applica&#146;s process of allocating the fair value to its tangible assets, primarily accounts
receivable, inventory and manufacturing property, plant and equipment in Mexico, and identifiable
intangible assets (i.e., customer relationships and tradenames) and liabilities, Applica determined
that the implied value of goodwill (the residual) was zero. The impairment relates primarily to
the Household Products segment.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>Other Intangible Assets</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In June&nbsp;1998, Applica acquired the household products group of The Black &#038; Decker Corporation
for $319.8&nbsp;million in cash and assumed certain related liabilities. As part of the acquisition,
Applica acquired the right to use the Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> trademark in four product categories: garment
care, cooking, food preparation and beverage. Applica has the right to sell products under this
license agreement in North America, Central America, and South America, excluding Brazil. The
excess purchase price consideration over the estimated fair value of the acquired net assets was
$228.8&nbsp;million, of which $47.2&nbsp;million was allocated to an intangible asset related to the Black &#038;
Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> trademark and the remaining $181.6&nbsp;million was allocated to goodwill. The $47.2&nbsp;million
assigned to the fair value of the intangible asset was based on a valuation performed by Applica,
with assistance from an independent third party valuation group, as of June&nbsp;1998. For the first
five years (through June&nbsp;30, 2003), the license was on a royalty free basis for the core product
categories identified above. Mutually agreed upon renewals would be at a stated royalty rate,
along with specified minimum royalty payments. The agreement provided for an initial period of
five years, with a subsequent five-year extended term and up to three additional five-year
extensions.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Beginning in 2002, sales of Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> branded products under the original four product
categories began to decrease significantly, which decrease continued through December&nbsp;31, 2003.
Sales of Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> branded products under the original four product categories for 2003,
2002, and 2001 were $399&nbsp;million, $429&nbsp;million and $446&nbsp;million, respectively. As a result of the
continuation of the lower sales of Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> branded products in the four core product
categories in the third and fourth quarter of 2003 (which are Applica&#146;s busiest quarters due to the
seasonality of its business), management determined that a triggering event had occurred in
accordance with SFAS 144, &#147;Accounting for the Impairment or Disposal of Long-Lived Assets&#148;.
Accordingly, Applica performed a valuation analysis by calculating the expected effective royalty
rate in 2004 through 2006, taking into account the minimum royalties as well as the projected sales
of Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> branded products under the original four categories. Based on the valuation
analysis, management believed that there was no remaining value in the initial intangible asset as
of December&nbsp;31, 2003. Accordingly, Applica recorded an
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">F-23
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE H &#151; ASSETS HELD FOR SALE</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In July&nbsp;2005, Applica decided to close its manufacturing facility in Mexico. The manufacturing
operations ceased production in October&nbsp;2005. In February&nbsp;2006, Applica entered into a sale and
purchase agreement with an unrelated third party to sell the land and building housing its factory
in Mexico for approximately $5.5&nbsp;million. The sale is expected to close in the second quarter of
2006. The land and building were classified as assets held for sale and included in prepaid
expenses and other in the accompanying consolidated balance sheet at a net realizable value of
approximately $5.3&nbsp;million (net of commissions) as of December&nbsp;31, 2005.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In connection with the abovementioned closure of the Mexico manufacturing facility, certain
machinery and equipment and furniture and fixtures totaling approximately $0.4&nbsp;million and certain
raw materials inventory totaling approximately $0.4&nbsp;million have been classified as assets held for
sale and included in prepaid expenses and other in the accompanying consolidated balance sheet as
of December&nbsp;31, 2005.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In connection with the 2004 restructuring plan to move the production of irons and toaster
ovens to third party suppliers in China, approximately $0.7&nbsp;million of machinery and equipment was
classified as assets held for sale and included in prepaid expenses and other in the accompanying
consolidated balance sheet at December&nbsp;31, 2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;All assets held for sale relate to the Manufacturing reportable segment.
</DIV>

<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">


<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 18pt"><B>Applica Incorporated and Subsidiaries<BR>
NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS &#151; CONTINUED</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE K &#151; SHORT-TERM DEBT</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In November&nbsp;2004, Applica amended and restated the senior credit facility, to, among other
things, reduce the credit amount to $175&nbsp;million (including $10.0&nbsp;million for letters of credit).
In June&nbsp;2005, Applica again amended its senior credit facility to provide a temporary increase in
liquidity from July through November&nbsp;2005. In December&nbsp;2005, Applica amended and restated the
senior credit facility to reduce the credit amount to $125&nbsp;million, reduce the applicable interest
rate margins, reduce the minimum daily and average monthly availability reserves, and add
receivables collected by its Hong Kong subsidiary to the collateral base. The amended credit
facility matures in November&nbsp;2009.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Advances under the credit facility are governed by Applica&#146;s collateral value, which is based
upon percentages of eligible accounts receivable and inventories. Under the amended facility, if
Applica does not maintain a minimum fixed charge coverage ratio of 1.0 to 1.0, Applica must
maintain a minimum daily availability under its borrowing base of $10&nbsp;million and a minimum average
monthly availability of $13&nbsp;million. If Applica maintains a fixed charge coverage ratio of greater
than 1.0 to 1.0, there is no availability requirement and no availability block. As of December
31, 2005, Applica&#146;s fixed charge coverage ratio was less than 1.0 to 1.0.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As of December&nbsp;31, 2005, Applica was borrowing approximately $69.5&nbsp;million under the facility
and had approximately $29.7&nbsp;million available for future cash borrowings, which is net of the $10
million daily block.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;At Applica&#146;s option, interest accrues on the loans made under the credit facility at either:
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt">
<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="font-size: 10pt">

<TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="7%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>LIBOR (adjusted for any reserves), plus a specified margin (determined by Applica&#146;s
average quarterly availability and set at 1.75% at December&nbsp;31, 2005), which was 6.14% at
December&nbsp;31, 2005; or</TD>
</TR>

<TR>
    <TD style="font-size: 6pt">&nbsp;</TD>
</TR><TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="7%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>the Base Rate (which is Bank of America&#146;s prime rate), plus a specified margin
(determined based upon Applica&#146;s average quarterly availability and was zero at December
31, 2005), which was 7.25% at December&nbsp;31, 2005.</TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; text-indent: 0%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Swing loans up to $15.0&nbsp;million bear interest at the Base Rate plus a specified margin (determined
based upon Applica&#146;s average quarterly availability and was zero at December&nbsp;31, 2005), which was
7.25% at December&nbsp;31, 2005.</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The credit facility is collateralized by substantially all of the real and personal property,
tangible and intangible, of Applica Incorporated and its domestic subsidiaries, as well as:
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt">
<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="font-size: 10pt">

<TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="7%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The credit facility is guaranteed by all of the current, and will be guaranteed by any future,
domestic subsidiaries of Applica Incorporated.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The credit facility contains a number of significant covenants that, among other things,
restrict the ability of Applica to dispose of assets, incur additional indebtedness, prepay other
indebtedness, pay dividends, repurchase or redeem capital stock, enter into certain investments or
create new subsidiaries, enter into sale and lease-back transactions, make certain acquisitions,
engage in mergers or consolidations, create liens, or engage in certain transactions with
affiliates, and that otherwise restrict corporate and business activities. At December&nbsp;31, 2005,
Applica was in compliance with all covenants under the credit facility.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As of December&nbsp;31, 2005, Applica had letters of credit of $1.7&nbsp;million outstanding under its
credit facility, as compared to $1.5&nbsp;million outstanding as of December&nbsp;31, 2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE L &#151; LONG-TERM DEBT</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>10% Senior Subordinated Notes</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica issued $130.0&nbsp;million in 10% Senior Subordinated Notes in July&nbsp;1998, which bear
interest at an annual rate of 10%, are payable semi-annually and mature on July&nbsp;31, 2008. The
notes are general unsecured obligations of Applica and rank subordinate in right of payment to all
senior debt of Applica and pari passu in right of payment to all future subordinated indebtedness
of Applica. The notes may be redeemed at the option of Applica, in whole or in part, at various
redemption prices.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The indenture pursuant to which the notes were issued contains certain covenants that, among
other things, limit the ability of Applica to incur additional indebtedness and issue preferred
stock, pay dividends or make other certain restricted payments, apply net proceeds from certain
asset sales, or sell stock of subsidiaries. At December&nbsp;31, 2005, Applica was in compliance with
all covenants under the subordinated note indenture.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;During 2003, Applica repurchased $65&nbsp;million of these notes and incurred a loss on redemption
of $3.9&nbsp;million. In February&nbsp;2004, Applica repurchased an additional $4.25&nbsp;million of these notes
and incurred a loss on redemption of $187,000. In October&nbsp;2005, Applica entered into a secured term
loan agreement with Mast Credit Opportunities I, (Master) Ltd. (&#147;Mast Credit&#148;) to borrow $20
million. Applica used a portion of the proceeds from the term loan to repurchase from Mast Credit
$5.0&nbsp;million of its 10% notes at 98% of par value. The repurchase of the notes resulted in a gain
on redemption of approximately $0.06&nbsp;million.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>Term Loan</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In October&nbsp;2005, Applica entered into a secured term loan agreement with Mast Credit
Opportunities I, (Master) Ltd. (&#147;Mast Credit&#148;) to borrow $20&nbsp;million. The term loan is secured by
a lien on Applica&#146;s assets, which is subordinate to Applica&#146;s senior revolving credit facility.
Applica used a portion of the proceeds from the term loan to repurchase from Mast Credit $5.0&nbsp;million of its 10%
senior subordinated notes due 2008 at 98% of par value. The
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">F-27
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 18pt"><B>Applica Incorporated and Subsidiaries<BR>
NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS &#151; CONTINUED</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; text-indent: 0%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">repurchase of the bonds resulted in a gain on redemption of approximately $0.06&nbsp;million. The
balance of the proceeds was used to pay down Applica&#146;s senior revolving credit facility. As
reported in a Schedule&nbsp;13G filed with the SEC on February&nbsp;13, 2006, Mast Credit owns approximately
9.6% of the outstanding common stock of Applica.</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The term loan bears interest at the three-month LIBOR rate plus 625 basis points, which was
10.9% at December&nbsp;31, 2005. The term loan matures in November&nbsp;2009 and requires no principal
payments until such time. Interest is paid monthly and in 2005, Applica recorded $425,000 in
interest expense associated with this loan. In connection with the repayment of the term loan,
after June&nbsp;30, 2006 Applica is required to pay an exit fee that increases on a periodic basis from
1% to 4% of the principal amount of the loan. Applica incurred fees of approximately $0.2&nbsp;million
in connection with the term loan.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The term loan contains the covenants similar to the senior credit facility, which are
discussed in Note K- Short-Term Debt, above. At December&nbsp;31, 2005, Applica was in compliance with
all covenants under the term loan. For additional information regarding this loan, see Note T &#151;
Related Party Transactions, below.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>Notes Payable</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In conjunction with the acquisition of Weitech, Inc. in May&nbsp;2002, Applica Consumer Products,
Inc., Applica&#146;s U.S. operating subsidiary, issued $3.0&nbsp;million of notes payable primarily to the
former shareholders of Weitech. These notes were unsecured, bore interest at an annual rate of
6.0%, and were paid off in June&nbsp;2005. At December&nbsp;31, 2004, the notes payable were included in
current portion of long term debt in the accompanying consolidated balance sheet.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica&#146;s long-term debt, including interest rate swaps designated as hedges, is summarized

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE M &#151; EMPLOYEE BENEFIT PLANS</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica has a 401(k) plan for its employees to which it makes discretionary contributions at
rates dependent on the level of each employee&#146;s contributions. Contributions made by Applica&#146;s
employees are limited to the maximum allowable for federal income tax purposes. The amounts
charged to earnings for this plan during the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2005, 2004 and 2003 totaled
approximately $650,000, $477,000 and $749,000,
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">F-28
</DIV>

NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS &#151; CONTINUED</B>

<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; text-indent: 0%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">respectively, and were included as a component of operating expenses in the consolidated

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE N &#151; INCOME TAXES</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;At December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004, Applica had deferred tax assets in excess of deferred tax
liabilities of $111.1&nbsp;million and $93.8&nbsp;million, respectively. Applica determined that it was more
likely than not that $10.4&nbsp;million and $11.2&nbsp;million of such assets will be realized, resulting in
a valuation allowance of $100.7&nbsp;million and $82.6&nbsp;million as of December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004,
respectively. Applica evaluates its ability to realize its deferred tax assets on a quarterly
basis and adjusts the amount of its valuation allowance, if necessary. Applica operates within
multiple taxing jurisdictions and is subject to audit in those jurisdictions. Because of the
complex issues involved, any claims can require an extended period to resolve.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Commencing in 2001, the earnings of certain foreign entities were taxed based on U.S.
statutory rates, notwithstanding the repatriation of earnings. Applica repatriated approximately
$91.4&nbsp;million of income from its foreign operations in 2004. In 2005, Applica did not repatriate
any income from its foreign operations. No provision was made for U.S. taxes on the remaining
accumulated undistributed earnings of Applica&#146;s foreign subsidiaries of approximately $52.9&nbsp;million
at December&nbsp;31, 2005 and $61.5&nbsp;million at December&nbsp;31, 2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;SFAS No.&nbsp;109, &#147;Accounting for Income Taxes&#148; requires that a valuation allowance be established
when it is more likely than not that all or a portion of a deferred tax asset will not be realized.
A review of all available positive and negative evidence needs to be considered, including a
company&#146;s current and past performance, the market environment in which the company operates, the
utilization of past tax credits and length of carryback and carryforward periods. Forming a
conclusion that a valuation allowance is not needed is difficult when there is negative objective
evidence such as cumulative losses in recent years. Cumulative losses weigh heavily in the overall
assessment.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica elected on its 2004 U.S. Corporation Income Tax Return to take advantage of new
section 965 of the Internal Revenue Code enacted as part of the American Jobs Creation Act of 2004.
In general, section 965(a) provides, for one taxable year, an 85% dividends-received deduction
with respect to certain cash dividends a company receives from its controlled foreign corporations.
Applica elected the 85% dividends-received deduction on $66.7&nbsp;million of cash dividends. The
related tax on the repatriated earnings was accrued in 2004. There has been no impact to the
consolidated financial statements for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2005 as a result of this election
except for an income tax benefit of approximately $0.6&nbsp;million recorded in the third quarter of
2005 pursuant to Notice 2005-64 issued by the IRS in September&nbsp;2005.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica expects to realize the benefits of the remaining net deferred tax assets of
approximately $10.4&nbsp;million as of December&nbsp;31, 2005, primarily from identified tax planning
strategies in the U.S. and Argentina, as well as projected taxable income from other countries.
Applica expects to continue to maintain a valuation allowance on certain future tax benefits until
an appropriate level of profitability is reached or Applica is able to develop tax strategies which
would enable it to conclude that it is more likely than not that a portion of its net deferred tax
assets would be realized.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In October&nbsp;2005, Applica was notified that Applica Canada Corporation, Applica&#146;s Canadian
operating subsidiary, was selected for an income tax audit for the years 2003 and 2004 by the
Canada Customs and Revenue Agency. In January&nbsp;2006, Applica was notified by the Internal Revenue
Service that it will be examining the years 2000, 2001 and 2002 in connection with U.S. loss
carryback claims that could result in a refund to Applica of $1.0&nbsp;million. Management believes
that adequate provision for taxes has been made for the years under examination.

against these foreign tax credits of $16.7
million and $14.7&nbsp;million in 2005 and 2004, respectively. Applica establishes valuation allowances
against the state NOLs and U.S. foreign tax credits when it is more likely than not that the
benefits will not be realized prior to expiration. Applica&#146;s U.S. subsidiaries have provided a
valuation allowance of $90.9&nbsp;million on all their deferred tax assets, except for $6.2&nbsp;million
related to net operating loss carryforwards that can be used as a result of a tax planning strategy
pursuant to SFAS 109.

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE O &#151; COMMITMENTS AND CONTINGENCIES</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>Litigation and Other Matters</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Shareholder Litigation</I></B>. Applica is a defendant in <U>Scott Schultz, individually and on
behalf of all others similarly situated, v. Applica Incorporated, Harry D. Schulman and Terry L.
Polistina</U>, 06-60149-CIV-DIMITROULEAS, which was filed in the United States District Court,
Southern District of Florida on February&nbsp;3, 2006, and <U>Joseph Rothman, individually and on
behalf of all others similarly situated, v. Applica Incorporated, Harry D. Schulman and Terry L.
Polistina</U>, 06-60230-CIV-ZLOCH, which was filed in the United States District Court, Southern
District of Florida on February&nbsp;24, 2006.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;These matters are purported class action complaints filed on behalf of purchasers of Applica
Incorporated common stock during the period between November&nbsp;4, 2004 and April&nbsp;28, 2005. The
complaints charge Applica and certain executive officers with violations of the Securities Exchange
Act of 1934. The complaints allege that, throughout the class period, Applica issued materially
false and misleading statements regarding its ability to transform its business and become more
profitable. The complaints claim that these statements were materially false and misleading on the
asserted basis that they failed to disclose the following: (a)&nbsp;that Applica was experiencing
decreasing demand for its products; in particular, demand for two key products, Tide&#153; Buzz&#153;
Ultrasonic Stain Remover and Home Cafe&#153; single cup coffee maker, were not meeting internal
expectations; (b)&nbsp;that Applica was materially overstating its net worth by failing to timely write
down the value of its inventory which had become obsolete and unsaleable; (c)&nbsp;that Applica was
experiencing higher product warranty returns, which it had not appropriately reserved for; and (d)
that Applica&#146;s financial statements issued during the class period were not prepared in accordance
with GAAP and therefore were materially false and misleading.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The plaintiffs seek, among other relief, to be declared a class, to be awarded compensatory
damages, rescission rights, unspecified damages and attorneys&#146; fees and costs. Applica believes
the claims are without merit. Applica intends to vigorously defend these lawsuits but may be
unable to successfully resolve the disputes without
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">F-31
</DIV>

loss cannot presently be determined with respect to these litigation matters.</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As is often the case, the SEC has requested that Applica voluntarily produce certain documents
in connection with an informal inquiry related to these matters. Applica intends to respond to the
requests for documents and are fully cooperating with the SEC in this matter.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Other Matters</I></B>. Applica is subject to legal proceedings, product liability claims and other
claims that arise in the ordinary course of its business. In the opinion of management, the amount
of ultimate liability with respect to such matters, if any, in excess of applicable insurance
coverage, is not likely to have a material effect on Applica&#146;s business, financial condition,
results of operations or liquidity. However, as the outcome of litigation or other claims is
difficult to predict, significant changes in the estimated exposures could occur.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As a distributor of consumer products, Applica is also subject to the Consumer Products Safety
Act, which empowers the Consumer Products Safety Commission (CPSC)&nbsp;to exclude from the market
products that are found to be unsafe or hazardous. Applica receives inquiries from the CPSC in the
ordinary course of its business. In the opinion of management, the amount of ultimate liability
with respect to such matters, if any, is not likely to have a material effect on Applica&#146;s
business, financial condition, results of operations or liquidity. However, under certain
circumstances, the CPSC could require Applica to repurchase or recall one or more of its products.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>Employment and Other Agreements</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica has entered into employment agreements with all three of its executive officers,
including the President and Chief Executive Officer. These contracts terminate on May&nbsp;1, 2007 and
provide the executives with the right to receive lump sum payments of 2.5 times (1.5 times for
employees other than the CEO) their severance base if their employment is terminated after a change
in control of Applica, as defined in such agreements. The term &#147;severance base&#148; is defined in the
agreement as the sum of (1)&nbsp;the executive&#146;s base salary, plus (2)&nbsp;the higher of (a)&nbsp;the
target-level incentive bonus for the year during which the termination occurs and (b)&nbsp;the average
of the incentive bonuses paid to the executive for the three years immediately preceding the year
in which the termination occurs. The agreements automatically extend for terms of one year unless
either party gives notice not less than 180&nbsp;days (30&nbsp;days for employees other than the CEO) prior
to the end of the then effective term.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica has also entered into change-in-control agreements and enhanced severance agreements
with certain of its employees.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Michael J. Michienzi elected to resign his position as President &#151; Household Products
Division of Applica Consumer Products, Inc. in December&nbsp;2005, at which time his employment
agreement terminated.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In December&nbsp;2005, Applica entered into an agreement with Alvarez &#038; Marsal, LLC, a global
professional services firm specializing in turnaround management, to work with the Board of
Directors and management team to identify additional actions to accelerate the Company&#146;s financial
turnaround. Over the past few months, Alvarez &#038; Marsal partnered with Applica&#146;s leadership team to
evaluate its strategic plan, implement various business initiatives and drive performance
improvement. David Coles of Alvarez &#038; Marsal is currently serving as interim Chief Operating
Officer of Applica. The term of the agreement has been extended to mid-May&nbsp;2006.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In June&nbsp;2005, Applica entered into a managed services agreement with Auxis, Inc., an
information technology services firm. Pursuant to such agreement, Auxis is responsible for
managing all of Applica&#146;s information technology infrastructure (including telecommunications,
networking, data centers and the help desk) in North America and China. The agreement is for a
term of four years and provides for payments of approximately $170,000 per month depending on the
services required by Applica. The agreement provides for early termination

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>License Agreements</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica licenses the Black &#038; Decker brand in North America, Latin America (excluding Brazil)
and the Caribbean for four core categories of household appliances: beverage products, food
preparation products, garment care products and cooking products. In October&nbsp;2004, Applica and
The Black &#038; Decker Corporation extended the trademark license agreement through December&nbsp;2010.
Under the agreement as extended, Applica agreed to continue to pay The Black &#038; Decker Corporation
royalties based on a percentage of sales, with guaranteed minimum royalty payments of $12.5&nbsp;million
annually. Renewals of the license agreement, if mutually agreed upon, will be for five-year
periods. If Black &#038; Decker does not agree to renew the license agreement, Applica has 18&nbsp;months to
transition out of the brand name. No minimum royalty payments will be due during such transition
period. The Black &#038; Decker Corporation has agreed not to compete in the four core product
categories for a period of five years after the termination of the license agreement.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Upon request, Black &#038; Decker may elect to extend the license to use the Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> brand
to certain additional products. In 2003, the license was further extended to certain electronic
pest and insect control products, an ultrasonic stain removing appliance and vacuum bag sealers.
In 2004, the license was extended to bug zappers. In 2005, the license was extended to electric
garment steamers and hair straightening and curling irons for the Latin American marketplace.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Further, Applica has a license from The Procter &#038; Gamble Company to use the Home Cafe&#153; brand
worldwide for the Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> single cup brewing machine.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">F-33
</DIV>

<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 18pt"><B>Applica Incorporated and Subsidiaries<BR>
NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS &#151; CONTINUED</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica owns the Littermaid<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> trademark for self-cleaning litter boxes and has extended the
trademark for accessories such as litter, a litterbox privacy tent and waste receptacles. Applica
owns two patents and has exclusive licenses to three other patents covering the Littermaid<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> litter
box, which require Applica to pay royalties based on a percentage of sales. The license agreements
are for the life of the applicable patent and do not require minimum royalty payments. The patents
have been issued in the United States and a number of foreign countries.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In April&nbsp;2004, Applica entered into a license and settlement agreement with Tilia, Inc. and
Tilia International, Inc. to resolve patent infringement litigation pending involving the Black &#038;
Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> Freshguard Vacuum Sealer and bags. As part of the license and settlement agreement,
Applica agreed to pay Tilia royalties for using its vacuum sealing technology. In addition,
Applica agreed to not contest the validity of Tilia&#146;s previously disputed patents and to settle any
future disputes over product infringement by arbitration.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>Supply Agreement</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In June&nbsp;2004, Applica entered into a supply agreement with Elec-Tech International (H.K.)
Company, Ltd. Elec-Tech accounted for approximately 35% of Applica&#146;s total purchases in 2005 and
approximately 14% of Applica&#146;s total purchases in 2004. No other supplier accounted for more than
10% of Applica&#146;s total purchases in 2005, 2004, and 2003.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;At December&nbsp;31, 2005, all significant accounts receivable from Elec-Tech had been settled. At
December&nbsp;31, 2005 accounts payable of $9.9&nbsp;million were due to Elec-Tech. At December&nbsp;31, 2004,
accounts receivable of $9.1&nbsp;million from Elec-Tech and accounts payable of $10.8&nbsp;million due to
Elec-Tech were presented net and were included in accounts payable in the accompanying consolidated
balance sheet.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In December&nbsp;2005, Applica terminated the supply agreement as the result of material breaches
by Elec-Tech, most of which were corrected after the termination date. After the termination,
Applica continued to purchase products from Elec-Tech and Elec-Tech continued to produce and ship
products to Applica.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>Agreement with Banc of America Securities</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica has an agreement with Banc of America Securities LLC for the provision of investment
banking services. Such agreement provides that upon the close of certain strategic transactions,
Applica will pay Banc of America a fee based on the aggregate consideration to be paid in any such
transaction.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE P &#151; SHAREHOLDERS&#146; EQUITY</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>Stock Options</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Under various plans, Applica may grant incentive or non-qualified stock options to employees
and directors. The terms of stock options granted under the plans are determined by the
Compensation Committee of the Board of Directors at the time of grant, including the exercise
price, term and any restrictions on the exercisability of such option. The exercise price of all
options granted under the plans equals the market price at the date of grant and no option is
exercisable after the expiration of ten years from the date of grant. The stock options
outstanding under the plans were generally granted for terms of five, six or ten years and vest on
a straight line basis over periods ranging from one to six years. No compensation expense was
recognized upon either the grant or exercise of these stock options, except for $0.1&nbsp;million during
2004 in connection with the resignation of the former Chairman of the Board.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As of December&nbsp;31, 2005, there are no shares available for grant under the 1988 Directors
Stock Option Plan or the 1992 Employees&#146; Incentive Stock Option Plan, 703 shares available for
grant under the 1996 Stock Option Plan, 730 shares available for grant under the 1998 Stock Option
Plan and 610,866 shares available for grant under the 2000 Stock Option Plan.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In the past, Applica has also granted non-qualified stock options that are not under any plan.
However, no non-plan options have been granted since 1998.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">F-34
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B>Employee Stock Purchase Plan</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In May&nbsp;2000, Applica&#146;s shareholders authorized up to 500,000 shares of common stock for the
2000 Employee Stock Purchase Plan. Under the plan, eligible employees could elect to participate
on January 1 or July 1 of each year. Subject to certain limitations determined in accordance with
calculations set forth in the plan, an eligible employee is granted a right to purchase shares of
common stock (up to a maximum of 1,000 shares) on the last business day on or before each June
30<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">th</SUP> and December&nbsp;31<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">st</SUP> of any year during which he or she is a participant.
The option exercise price per share was an amount equal to 85% of the lower of the market price on
the first day of the twelve-month offering period or the market price on the exercise date, unless
the participant&#146;s entry date is not the first day of the offering period, in which case the
exercise price will be an amount equal to 85% of the lower of the market price of the common stock
on the entry date or the market price on the exercise date. As of December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004, an
aggregate of 350,142 and 310,997 shares, respectively, of common stock had been issued under the
plan. In September&nbsp;2005, the Compensation Committee of the Board of Directors elected to terminate
the Employee Stock Purchase Plan effective December&nbsp;31, 2005. Therefore, no additional shares will
be issued under such plan.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE Q &#151; VENDOR CONSIDERATION</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Applica&#146;s supplier of vacuum packaging products has agreed to reimburse it $4.0&nbsp;million for a
portion of the costs of litigating a patent infringement matter with Tilia International, Inc. The
reimbursement is evidenced by an unsecured note receivable bearing interest at a rate of 6% per
annum, which is payable in equal quarterly installments of $200,000, plus interest, over five
years. Management believes that the ultimate collection of the note
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt">F-35
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always">&nbsp;</H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 18pt"><B>Applica Incorporated and Subsidiaries<BR>
NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS &#151; CONTINUED</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="margin-left: 0%; text-indent: 0%; margin-right: 0%; font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">receivable is not assured and is dependent on Applica&#146;s future purchases from the supplier.
Therefore, the amount collected on the note receivable is being recorded on the &#147;cash basis&#148; over
the five-year period as a reduction of future product costs. Applica collected cash of $228,000 and
$380,000 from the supplier in 2005 and 2004, respectively, and recorded it as a reduction of cost
of goods sold in each such year. Applica does not expect purchases from the supplier to be
significant going forward, and therefore, the collectibility of the note receivable is highly
unlikely.</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE R &#151; BUSINESS SEGMENT AND GEOGRAPHIC AREA INFORMATION</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Statement of Financial Accounting Standards No.&nbsp;131, &#147;Disclosure about Segments of an
Enterprise and Related Information,&#148; established standards for reporting information about business
segments in annual financial statements. It also established standards for related disclosures
about products and services, major customers and geographic areas. Business segments are defined as
components of a business about which separate financial information is available that is evaluated
regularly by the chief operating decision maker, or decision making group, in deciding how to
allocate resources and in assessing performance.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Through October&nbsp;2005, Applica managed its operations through three business segments:
Household Products, Professional Personal Care Products and Manufacturing. The Manufacturing
segment ceased operations in October&nbsp;2005 and for the remainder of the year, Applica managed its
operations through two business segments: Household Products and Professional Personal Care
Products.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt">
<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="font-size: 10pt">

<TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="7%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>The Household Products segment is a leading distributor and marketer of small
electric household appliances, primarily cooking, garment care, food preparation,
beverage products, pet products and pest products, marketed under the licensed brand
names, such as Black &#038; Decker<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> and Home Caf&#233;&#153;, as well as owned brand names, such as
Applica<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP>, Gizmo<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP>, Littermaid<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> and Windmere<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP>. The Household Products segment sales are
handled primarily through in house sales representatives and are made to mass
merchandisers, specialty retailers and appliance distributors in North America, Latin
America and the Caribbean.</TD>
</TR>

<TR>
    <TD style="font-size: 6pt">&nbsp;</TD>
</TR><TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="7%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>The Professional Personal Care segment is a distributor and marketer of a broad
range of personal care products under the Belson<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> and Jerdon<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> brands. The Professional
Personal Care segment sales are handled primarily through independent sales
representatives to specialty retailers and some mass merchandisers in North America.
The Jerdon<SUP style="font-size: 85%; vertical-align: text-top">&#174;</SUP> brand was sold in October&nbsp;2004.</TD>
</TR>

<TR>
    <TD style="font-size: 6pt">&nbsp;</TD>
</TR><TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="7%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left"><B>&#149;</B></TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>The Manufacturing segment consisted of Applica&#146;s Mexican and Hong Kong-based
manufacturing operations. The Mexican manufacturing operations ceased production in
October&nbsp;2005. In July&nbsp;2004, Applica sold all of its interest in its Hong Kong
manufacturing operations.</TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The profitability measure employed by Applica for making decisions about allocating resources
to segments and evaluating segment performance is operating (loss)&nbsp;earnings. Generally, segments
follow the same accounting policies as those described in Note A of Applica&#146;s consolidated
financial statements.
</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 18pt"><B>Applica Incorporated and Subsidiaries<BR>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The following table sets forth the approximate amounts and percentages of Applica&#146;s
consolidated net sales by product category during the periods ending December&nbsp;31,:
</DIV>
<DIV align="center">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In 2005 and 2004, Applica&#146;s international operations were conducted primarily in China and
Mexico, with lesser activities in Canada, the Caribbean and South and Central America. In July
2004, Applica sold its Hong Kong-based manufacturing operations. As a result, Applica&#146;s
international operations are now conducted primarily in Latin America, Canada and the Caribbean.
Other than the United States, Applica does not have external sales to customers located in any
country which exceed 10% of consolidated sales. The following table sets forth the composition of
Applica&#146;s sales between those in the United States and those in other locations for each year:
</DIV>
